# 📚 AI Agents x Science of Science

[![Daily Paper Update](https://github.com/liu-yi-xuan/arxiv-agent-science-of-science/actions/workflows/daily_update.yml/badge.svg)](https://github.com/liu-yi-xuan/arxiv-agent-science-of-science/actions/workflows/daily_update.yml)

> **Why these two fields belong together:** AI agents are transforming how science is done — automating literature review, generating hypotheses, running experiments, and writing papers. At the same time, Science of Science provides the quantitative lens to understand how the agent research revolution itself unfolds — tracking knowledge diffusion, measuring impact, and mapping collaboration. They form a feedback loop: agents accelerate science, and science of science helps us build better agents.

This repo auto-updates daily from ArXiv, tracking papers at the intersection of AI agents and the scientific research process.

---

## 🔖 Categories

| Category | Description |
|----------|-------------|
| 🔬 **AI Agents for Scientific Discovery** | Agents that do science — hypothesis generation, experiment design, lab automation, drug discovery, mathematical reasoning |
| 📖 **Research Workflow Agents** | Agents for literature review, paper writing, peer review assistance, knowledge extraction, and research summarization |
| 🧠 **Multi-Agent Scientific Collaboration** | Multi-agent systems that simulate or enhance research teams, collaborative discovery, and scientific debate |
| 📊 **Computational Science of Science** | Studying research computationally — citation dynamics, knowledge diffusion, research trends, scientometrics, impact prediction |
| 🛠️ **Benchmarks & Evaluation for Research Agents** | Measuring agent capabilities in scientific/research contexts, reproducibility, and research quality assessment |

---

## ⭐ Classic & Notable Papers

### 🔬 AI Agents for Scientific Discovery

| Year | Title | TLDR |
|------|-------|------|
| 2023 | [ChemCrow: Augmenting large-language models with chemistry tools](https://arxiv.org/abs/2304.05376) | An LLM agent equipped with 17 chemistry tools that can plan and execute chemical research tasks like drug discovery and materials design autonomously. |
| 2024 | [The AI Scientist: Towards Fully Automated Open-Ended Scientific Discovery](https://arxiv.org/abs/2408.06292) | First end-to-end system where AI agents independently generate research ideas, run experiments, write papers, and conduct peer review. |
| 2024 | [SciAgents: Automating scientific discovery through multi-agent intelligent graph reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2409.05556) | Multi-agent system using knowledge graphs to autonomously generate and validate scientific hypotheses across domains. |
| 2023 | [Voyager: An Open-Ended Embodied Agent with Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2305.16291) | First LLM-powered lifelong learning agent that builds a reusable skill library and continuously explores — a paradigm for open-ended discovery. |
| 2024 | [OS-Copilot: Towards Generalist Computer Agents with Self-Improvement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.07456) | A framework for building generalist agents that interact with any OS element and self-improve through experience — foundational for research automation. |
| 2024 | [SWE-agent: Agent-Computer Interfaces Enable Automated Software Engineering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2405.15793) | Designs agent-computer interfaces that let LLMs autonomously fix real GitHub issues — a model for agents that interact with research codebases. |
| 2025 | [Agent S2: An Agentic System for Compositional Generalization in Computer Use](https://arxiv.org/abs/2504.00906) | A compositional agent system that generalizes across computer tasks through hierarchical planning — applicable to complex scientific workflows. |

### 📖 Research Workflow Agents

| Year | Title | TLDR |
|------|-------|------|
| 2023 | [HuggingGPT: Solving AI Tasks with ChatGPT and its Friends in Hugging Face](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.17580) | Uses ChatGPT as a controller to orchestrate existing AI models for complex tasks — a blueprint for agents that chain research tools. |
| 2023 | [Reflexion: Language Agents with Verbal Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2303.11366) | Agents learn from mistakes via natural language self-reflection — key for iterative research workflows where agents refine hypotheses. |
| 2023 | [Can Large Language Models Provide Useful Feedback on Research Papers?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.01783) | Evaluates GPT-4's peer review-like feedback, finding substantial overlap with human reviewers — agents entering the review process. |
| 2023 | [Large Language Models for Science: A Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2312.07622) | Comprehensive survey of how LLMs transform scientific workflows — from literature analysis to hypothesis generation and experiment design. |
| 2024 | [AutoGen: Enabling Next-Gen LLM Applications via Multi-Agent Conversation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.08155) | A framework for multi-agent conversations — applicable to research brainstorming, collaborative writing, and iterative analysis. |
| 2024 | [OpenAgents: An Open Platform for Language Agents in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.10634) | Deploys three real-world agents (data, plugin, web) bridging agent research and everyday use — including data analysis for researchers. |
| 2024 | [ResearchAgent: Iterative Research Idea Generation over Scientific Literature](https://arxiv.org/abs/2404.07738) | An agent that iteratively reads papers, identifies gaps, and generates novel research ideas with supporting evidence. |

| 2023 | [ChatDev: Communicative Agents for Software Development](https://arxiv.org/abs/2307.07924) | Multi-agent chat chain with specialized roles (CEO, CTO, programmer) — a template for multi-agent research teams (PI, reviewer, analyst). |
| 2023 | [MetaGPT: Meta Programming for Multi-Agent Collaboration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2308.00352) | Encodes SOPs into multi-agent workflows with structured outputs — applicable to research pipelines with defined methodology steps. |

| 2024 | [Debating with More Persuasive LLMs Leads to More Truthful Answers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.06782) | Multi-agent debate improves factual accuracy — directly applicable to scientific peer review and hypothesis validation. |

## 🏗️ Project Structure

```
├── README.md              # This file — auto-updated daily
├── fetch_papers.py        # ArXiv paper fetcher + classifier + TLDR generator
├── papers/                # Archive of daily paper fetches (JSON)
├── .github/workflows/     # GitHub Actions for daily automation
└── requirements.txt       # Python dependencies
```

## 🚀 How It Works

1. **Daily at 08:00 UTC**, a GitHub Action triggers `fetch_papers.py`
2. The script queries ArXiv for recent papers at the intersection of AI agents and science of science
3. Papers are classified into categories and a TLDR is generated
4. The README is auto-updated with the day's findings
5. Changes are committed and pushed automatically

Found a great paper at the intersection of agents and science? Open an issue or PR!
